# Component certificate: g6.ml.adapt_automl

Issuer state: qualified-draft.

Checks apply only to the recorded bytes, source cases and seeded inputs.
A detached SSH signature, when one is given, covers the certificate's content digest (this record without its history positions) in namespace g6-certificate.

Case values were transcribed by Codex (certificate step 2) and Claude Opus 5.5 (certificate v2a builder) and checked against the source by the independent pass (Claude Sonnet 5.5, a read-only independent reader (not the author of the code, the register or the cases)); the issuing tool also searched the source's text for each printed value (printed_values in the record).

## Rules by tier

- accuracy: computes
- balanced_accuracy: property (no printed value is checked; the independent reader's attestation: I read all of 3.4.4.4 in the held page: it prints a formula, the 1/n_classes limit and references, and no numerical example, so no printed value exists to run G6's balanced_accuracy on.)
- baseline: property (no printed value is checked; the independent reader's attestation: 3.4.8 prints 0.63, 0.579 and 0.94 for iris with labels changed in code and a split from train_test_split(random_state=0); neither the labels nor the split are printed, so G6's baseline cannot be run on printed inputs.)
- bounds: guard (G6's own threshold: no source sets it) (its code does only what its effect says, by the independent reader's attestation: _bounds only raises INPUT_INVALID or TOO_LARGE through bounded() and a class count; it changes nothing.)
- cv_training_part: property (no printed value is checked; the independent reader's attestation: Section 3.1 prints cross_val_score values only for the iris data loaded from scikit-learn (the data itself is not printed) with its own splits, so no printed value can be reproduced by running G6's cross_validate on printed inputs; the rule is a procedure (fit on k-1 folds of the training part, score the remaining one, the held-out rows never read), and the held-out rows reach the child only as unlabelled features used after the choice.)
- grouped: computes
- grouped_hold_out: property (no printed value is checked; the independent reader's attestation: 3.1.2.4 prints only GroupKFold test-fold indices, which test the folds (rule grouped), never a hold-out split; the hold-out is chosen from the rows' own hash by make_split, which the page cannot print, so no printed value exists that make_split can be run on.)
- headline_imbalance: property (no printed value is checked; the independent reader's attestation: 3.4.4.4 prints no number about when to prefer balanced accuracy and no example; the 10% threshold is G6's own and no printed value exists that G6's class_balance can be run on.) (G6's own choice: no source sets it)
- held_out: computes
- held_out_from_rows: guard (G6's own threshold: no source sets it) (its code does only what its effect says, by the independent reader's attestation: _caution is a pure string builder: it reads the count and returns text, saying 'optimistic' once the count is 1 or more; it writes nothing and gates nothing. The recording of each scoring (held_out_use, a small SQLite write under G6_HOME) is in another function the certificate's cases do not run; it is declared in the rule and its docstring says it is never a gate.)
- interval: guard (G6's own threshold: no source sets it) (its code does only what its effect says, by the independent reader's attestation: compare() sets clearly_better true only when the lift's lower end is above zero and adds a note when the score is undefined or too few resamples are defined; the red card for 'does not clearly beat' is chosen by said(), not here. compare() also computes the headline numbers (held_out, baseline, lift, gap), which are not part of this rule and rest on the metric rules.)
- leakage_probes: guard (G6's own threshold: no source sets it) (its code does only what its effect says, by the independent reader's attestation: _leakage_findings only builds a list of findings (copy, correlation, single-feature); prepare() turns a non-empty list into the LEAKAGE refusal. It changes no row, feature or score.)
- mae: computes
- min_held_out: guard (G6's own threshold: no source sets it) (its code does only what its effect says, by the independent reader's attestation: prepare() only refuses (seed, bounds, columns, class thinness, held-out size, class on both sides, duplicates, leakage) or returns the split, evidence and class balance; the refusals not named in this rule (seed, columns, duplicates across the split) are separate refusals, none lowers or changes a result.)
- overfit_gap: guard (G6's own threshold: no source sets it) (its code does only what its effect says, by the independent reader's attestation: _gap_note is a pure function: it returns an extra sentence when the gap is 0.10 or more and the headline is higher-is-better, and an empty string otherwise; it refuses, scores and picks nothing. The refusal (_too_good) and the card now stay in said(), outside the checked function.)
- preprocessing_in_pipeline: property (no printed value is checked; the independent reader's attestation: The only printed values in 12.2 (0.76, 0.5, 0.43) come from SelectKBest and HistGradientBoostingClassifier on seeded random data split by train_test_split; G6's pipeline has no feature-selection step, imputes, scales and one-hot encodes, and splits by row hash, so no printed value is something G6's _pipeline can be run to reproduce or contradict.) (G6's own choice: no source sets it)
- r2: computes
- rmse: computes
- search_training_part: property (no printed value is checked; the independent reader's attestation: Section 3.1 prints no value for a search over settings: its printed numbers are cross_val_score results for iris loaded from scikit-learn and a split from train_test_split, neither reproducible by running G6's search on printed inputs, and the page does not describe a FLAML search; the rule is a procedure (candidates compared by k-fold on the training part only, the held-out rows scoring the chosen one once).)
- seed: property (no printed value is checked; the independent reader's attestation: The one printed value (0.84 in 12.3.3.1) comes from make_classification and train_test_split sharing one RandomState instance across calls; G6 passes an integer to each object and splits by hash, so no printed value is something G6's _estimator can be run to reproduce.)
- stratified: computes
- too_good: guard (G6's own threshold: no source sets it) (its code does only what its effect says, by the independent reader's attestation: _too_good returns nothing or raises the LEAKAGE MLError carrying the evidence; it computes nothing and changes no value.)

## What was not checked

A deliberately deceptive implementation shares the child process with the harness; the independent code review is what covers that limitation.

sklearn-1.9-cv-3.1, sklearn-1.9-cv-3.1.2.2, sklearn-1.9-cv-3.1.2.4, sklearn-1.9-metrics-3.4.4.2, sklearn-1.9-metrics-3.4.4.4, sklearn-1.9-metrics-3.4.6.1, sklearn-1.9-metrics-3.4.6.2, sklearn-1.9-metrics-3.4.6.3, sklearn-1.9-metrics-3.4.8, sklearn-1.9-pitfalls-12.2, sklearn-1.9-pitfalls-12.3.3.1: their full bytes were hashed by the issuing tool at issue; checks without the bytes recompute only the clause hash and trust this unsigned record. Nothing here shows that the bytes were held beyond that record, or that the source's URL still serves them.

- Issuer state qualified-draft: these checks were frozen after the code they check existed (a retrofit), so by the issuer's freeze rule they can reach qualified-draft at most. A named gap holds it at qualified-draft at most: a threshold these rules apply is G6's own choice, which no source sets: headline_imbalance, preprocessing_in_pipeline. A second route and invariants check these rules, and no value their source prints, by the independent reader's attestation (property-checked, no printed value: balanced_accuracy, baseline, cv_training_part, grouped_hold_out, headline_imbalance, preprocessing_in_pipeline, search_training_part, seed).
- These inputs are the ml kernel's, shared with adapt_sklearn (checks.json inherits its certificate folder, bound by the SHA-256 of its inputs); this certificate covers them on adapt_automl's own artifact, and adds the search.
- held_out checks printed sample counts on distinct row indices, not an iris model score. stratified applies the printed first-fold counts as a one-third hold-out; it does not claim the identical random split. grouped checks the printed test folds (sorted, and in the printed order at seed 0, where G6's seeded shuffle deals them so), and the case author's reading of them; grouped_hold_out checks the hold-out split's keeping of identical rows on one side.
- Property-checked, because the source prints no worked value G6's functions can be run on: search_training_part calls FLAML's search() directly with one fixed dummy-regressor candidate, one trial and no search dimensions, twice with different held-out rows, comparing its training-fold scores with an independent fold mean absolute error and checking that the held-out rows change none of them. cv_training_part is checked through the rank path on cross_validate, the function the search calls for every trial. preprocessing_in_pipeline fits through _fit.fit_predict (the search's final refit) twice with different held-out rows and compares the fitted transformation of the training rows. seed checks the integer random_state passed to an actual G6-created decision tree. baseline compares majority and mean predictions: the source prints 0.579, its most-frequent baseline's score, for a split of the bundled iris data whose labels it does not print, so there is no printed value G6's baseline function can be run on. balanced_accuracy and headline_imbalance compare independent computations; the 10% headline threshold is G6's own choice (headline_imbalance is marked own_choice, which holds this certificate at qualified-draft).
- The source's caveat that an integer seed 'may lead to less robust cross-validation results' (12.3.3.2) is not checked, and the results do not say so, though it matters most here: the search compares settings under one integer random_state.
- rmse is squared for the printed mean squared error and compared directly with an independent square root on generated inputs. The constant-target R2 case checks only that R2 is reported undefined; generated and boundary inputs include constant non-integer targets.
- Each guard fires at its threshold, holds just inside it and fires on its recall set; G6's removal of it breaks its firing case, and a seeded change of its threshold or of a comparison at it is caught. What else its code does rests on the independent reader's word (effect_only). Their thresholds are G6's own. Of interval, only the claim it gates is checked, not the interval's own values. held_out_from_rows checks the caution made from a count, not the count kept under G6_HOME.
- Competitive search, budget exhaustion and the equal shares of the trial cap and time budget between several candidates, all search spaces, the block's search orchestration, child_main file transport and the backend's further-process launch remain outside scope; so do the caller rules search_bounds and limits (the typed input's ranges and the process deadline), categorical one-hot encoding and its 50-category cap, all estimator classes and model-selection quality.

  "not_checked": [
    "Issuer state qualified-draft: these checks were frozen after the code they check existed (a retrofit), so by the issuer's freeze rule they can reach qualified-draft at most. A named gap holds it at qualified-draft at most: a threshold these rules apply is G6's own choice, which no source sets: headline_imbalance, preprocessing_in_pipeline. A second route and invariants check these rules, and no value their source prints, by the independent reader's attestation (property-checked, no printed value: balanced_accuracy, baseline, cv_training_part, grouped_hold_out, headline_imbalance, preprocessing_in_pipeline, search_training_part, seed).",
    "These inputs are the ml kernel's, shared with adapt_sklearn (checks.json inherits its certificate folder, bound by the SHA-256 of its inputs); this certificate covers them on adapt_automl's own artifact, and adds the search.",
    "held_out checks printed sample counts on distinct row indices, not an iris model score. stratified applies the printed first-fold counts as a one-third hold-out; it does not claim the identical random split. grouped checks the printed test folds (sorted, and in the printed order at seed 0, where G6's seeded shuffle deals them so), and the case author's reading of them; grouped_hold_out checks the hold-out split's keeping of identical rows on one side.",
    "Property-checked, because the source prints no worked value G6's functions can be run on: search_training_part calls FLAML's search() directly with one fixed dummy-regressor candidate, one trial and no search dimensions, twice with different held-out rows, comparing its training-fold scores with an independent fold mean absolute error and checking that the held-out rows change none of them. cv_training_part is checked through the rank path on cross_validate, the function the search calls for every trial. preprocessing_in_pipeline fits through _fit.fit_predict (the search's final refit) twice with different held-out rows and compares the fitted transformation of the training rows. seed checks the integer random_state passed to an actual G6-created decision tree. baseline compares majority and mean predictions: the source prints 0.579, its most-frequent baseline's score, for a split of the bundled iris data whose labels it does not print, so there is no printed value G6's baseline function can be run on. balanced_accuracy and headline_imbalance compare independent computations; the 10% headline threshold is G6's own choice (headline_imbalance is marked own_choice, which holds this certificate at qualified-draft).",
    "The source's caveat that an integer seed 'may lead to less robust cross-validation results' (12.3.3.2) is not checked, and the results do not say so, though it matters most here: the search compares settings under one integer random_state.",
    "rmse is squared for the printed mean squared error and compared directly with an independent square root on generated inputs. The constant-target R2 case checks only that R2 is reported undefined; generated and boundary inputs include constant non-integer targets.",
    "Each guard fires at its threshold, holds just inside it and fires on its recall set; G6's removal of it breaks its firing case, and a seeded change of its threshold or of a comparison at it is caught. What else its code does rests on the independent reader's word (effect_only). Their thresholds are G6's own. Of interval, only the claim it gates is checked, not the interval's own values. held_out_from_rows checks the caution made from a count, not the count kept under G6_HOME.",
    "Competitive search, budget exhaustion and the equal shares of the trial cap and time budget between several candidates, all search spaces, the block's search orchestration, child_main file transport and the backend's further-process launch remain outside scope; so do the caller rules search_bounds and limits (the typed input's ranges and the process deadline), categorical one-hot encoding and its 50-category cap, all estimator classes and model-selection quality."
  ],

  "register": [
    {
      "clause": "Section 3.1, opening paragraphs: learning the parameters of a prediction function and testing it on the same data is a methodological mistake, so part of the data is held out as a test set; when settings are tuned, knowledge of the test set can leak into the model, so a test set is still held out for final evaluation and settings are compared by cross-validation on the training set (k-fold: a model trained on k - 1 folds and validated on the remaining one).",
      "clause_sha256": "fb7ed739181097389a97a61680e0266bdf335ff62a93c225a45c3e35d529a6b0",

    "baseline": {
      "effect": "",
      "implements": "g6/ml/_metrics.py:baseline",
      "kind": "register",
      "own_choice": false,
      "rule": "a baseline that ignores the features (the most frequent training class, or the mean training target) is scored on the same held-out rows; a tie between classes goes to the first label in text order (G6's own; the source is silent on ties)",
      "source": "sklearn-1.9-metrics-3.4.8",
      "tier": "property"
    },
    "bounds": {
      "effect": "refuse",
      "implements": "g6/ml/_types.py:_bounds",
      "kind": "own",
      "own_choice": false,
      "rule": "a request is refused before anything is prepared when a row has a column that is neither the target nor a feature, a text cell holds more than 10,000 characters, the text cells hold more than 5,000,000 characters in all, or a classification target has more than 100 classes; the rows to predict are held to the same column and text limits",
      "source": "g6",
      "tier": "guard"
    },
    "cv_training_part": {
      "effect": "",
      "implements": "g6/ml/_fit.py:cross_validate",
      "kind": "register",
      "own_choice": false,
      "rule": "candidates are compared by k-fold cross-validation inside the training part only; the held-out rows score the chosen candidate once and choose nothing",
      "source": "sklearn-1.9-cv-3.1",
      "tier": "property"
    },
    "grouped": {
      "effect": "",
      "implements": "g6/ml/_fit.py:folds",
      "kind": "register",
      "own_choice": false,
      "rule": "rows with identical features form one group and stay on one side of every fold (G6's grouping of identical rows; the source groups dependent samples), the folds dealt in an order the seed shuffles",
      "source": "sklearn-1.9-cv-3.1.2.4",
      "tier": "computes"
    },
    "grouped_hold_out": {
      "effect": "",
      "implements": "g6/ml/_types.py:make_split",
      "kind": "register",
      "own_choice": false,
      "rule": "rows with identical features also stay on one side of the hold-out split, so no held-out row repeats a training row (G6's grouping of identical rows; the source groups dependent samples)",
      "source": "sklearn-1.9-cv-3.1.2.4",
      "tier": "property"
    },
    "headline_imbalance": {
      "effect": "",
      "implements": "g6/ml/_types.py:class_balance",
      "kind": "register",
      "own_choice": true,
      "rule": "the headline is balanced accuracy when the smallest class holds under 10% of the training rows (a threshold of G6's own; the held-out labels choose nothing), accuracy otherwise; both are always reported",
      "source": "sklearn-1.9-metrics-3.4.4.4",
      "tier": "property"
    },
    "held_out": {
      "effect": "",
      "implements": "g6/ml/_types.py:make_split",
      "kind": "register",
      "own_choice": false,
      "rule": "a score is measured on rows held out of training, which the extension chooses itself from the rows' own hash, so the same rows always give the same held-out rows whatever the seed",
      "source": "sklearn-1.9-cv-3.1",
      "tier": "computes"
    },
    "held_out_from_rows": {
      "effect": "note",
      "implements": "g6/ml/_report.py:_caution",
      "kind": "own",
      "own_choice": false,
      "rule": "each scoring of a held-out set is recorded by its hash, and every scored reply says how many times it was scored before; once a set has been scored before, the reply says a result kept after several tries on it is optimistic",
      "source": "g6",
      "tier": "guard"
    },
    "interval": {
      "effect": "note",
      "implements": "g6/ml/_metrics.py:compare",
      "kind": "own",
      "own_choice": false,
      "rule": "the model is said to beat the baseline only when the lower end of the lift's 95% percentile interval is above zero; the interval comes from 1,000 seeded resamples of the held-out rows by group (rows with identical features drawn together: a cluster bootstrap), fewer when resamples times held-out rows would pass 5,000,000, as the interval says; otherwise the result says the model does not clearly beat the baseline",
      "source": "g6",
      "tier": "guard"
    },
    "leakage_probes": {
      "effect": "refuse",
      "implements": "g6/ml/_types.py:_leakage_findings",
      "kind": "own",
      "own_choice": false,
      "rule": "a feature equal to the target, with Pearson or Spearman |r| of 0.99 or more against a numeric target (or a two-class numeric classification target) on the training rows, or predicting it alone for every row from the others (leave-one-out) at 0.99 or better and above a baseline, is refused as a suspected leak; the probes need at least 5 rows (10 for the one-feature probe) before they find anything (thresholds of G6's own)",
      "source": "g6",
      "tier": "guard"
    },
    "limits": {
      "effect": "",
      "implements": "",
      "kind": "caller",
      "own_choice": false,
      "rule": "the other sizes and limits one request may carry are the caller's within the typed input's ranges (20,000 rows, 200 features, 1,000 rows to predict, a fit deadline timeout_s of at most 600 s, each estimator's parameters within their ranges), refused beyond them before anything is prepared; the fit's process is stopped at its deadline and its memory capped at 1,536 MB",
